<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt2" typeof="mw:Transclusion" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"for","href":"./Template:For"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"a list of capitol buildings"},"2":{"wt":"List of state and territorial capitols in the United States"}},"i":0}}]}' id="mwAw"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1353705441" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt3" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'>.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+span.mw-empty-elt+.hatnote,.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .hatnote{display:none!important}}</style></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt2" id="mwBA">For a list of capitol buildings, see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./List_of_state_and_territorial_capitols_in_the_United_States" title="List of state and territorial capitols in the United States">List of state and territorial capitols in the United States</a>.</div>
<p class="mw-empty-elt" id="mwBQ"><span typeof="mw:Nowiki mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Use American English","href":"./Template:Use_American_English"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"June 2025"}},"i":0}}]}' id="mwBg"></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Use_American_English_from_June_2025" about="#mwt4"/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_Wikipedia_articles_written_in_American_English" about="#mwt4" id="mwBw"/>
<span typeof="mw:Nowiki mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt5" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Use mdy dates","href":"./Template:Use_mdy_dates"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"September 2019"}},"i":0}}]}' id="mwCA"></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Use_mdy_dates_from_September_2019" about="#mwt5" id="mwCQ"/></p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwCg"><a href="./File:US_map_-_states_and_capitals.png" class="mw-file-description" id="mwCw"><img resource="./File:US_map_-_states_and_capitals.png" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/US_map_-_states_and_capitals.png/250px-US_map_-_states_and_capitals.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="5499" data-file-height="4249" data-file-type="bitmap" height="193" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/US_map_-_states_and_capitals.png/500px-US_map_-_states_and_capitals.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwDA"/></a><figcaption id="mwDQ">U.S. states with their capitals</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwDg">This is a list of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Capital_city" title="Capital city" id="mwDw">capital cities</a> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./United_States" title="United States" id="mwEA">United States</a>, including places that serve or have served as federal, state, insular area, territorial, colonial and Native American capitals.</p>

<p id="mwEQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Washington,_D.C." title="Washington, D.C." id="mwEg">Washington, D.C.</a> has been the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Federal_capital" title="Federal capital" id="mwEw">federal capital</a> of the United States since 1800. Each <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./U.S._state" title="U.S. state" id="mwFA">U.S. state</a> has its own capital city, as do many of its <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Insular_area" title="Insular area" id="mwFQ">insular areas</a>. Most states have not changed their capital city since becoming a state, but the capital cities of their respective preceding colonies, territories, kingdoms, and republics typically changed multiple times. There have also been other governments within the current borders of the United States with their own capitals, such as the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Republic_of_Texas" title="Republic of Texas" id="mwFg">Republic of Texas</a>, Native American nations, and other unrecognized governments.</p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" data-mw='{"autoGenerated":true}' id="mwGQ"/></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwGg"><h2 id="National_capitals">National capitals</h2>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt14" typeof="mw:Transclusion" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"See also","href":"./Template:See_also"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"#Colonies of British America"}},"i":0}}]}' id="mwGw"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1353705441" about="#mwt15" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt14" id="mwHA">See also: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./#Colonies_of_British_America" title="">§<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>Colonies of British America</a></div>

<p id="mwHQ">The buildings in cities identified in the chart below served either as official capitals of the United States under the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Constitution_of_the_United_States" title="Constitution of the United States" id="mwHg">United States Constitution</a>, or, prior to its ratification, sites where the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Second_Continental_Congress" title="Second Continental Congress" id="mwHw">Second Continental Congress</a> or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Congress_of_the_Confederation" title="Congress of the Confederation" id="mwIA">Congress of the Confederation</a> met. The United States did not have a permanent capital under the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Articles_of_Confederation" title="Articles of Confederation" id="mwIQ">Articles of Confederation</a>.</p>

<p id="mwIg">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Constitution_of_the_United_States" title="Constitution of the United States" id="mwIw">U.S. Constitution</a> was ratified in 1787, and gave the Congress the power to exercise "exclusive legislation" over a district that "may, by Cession of particular States, and the acceptance of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./United_States_Congress" title="United States Congress" id="mwJA">Congress</a>, become the Seat of the Government of the United States."<sup about="#mwt18" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-1"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-1" id="mwJQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwJg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwJw">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKA">]</span></span></a></sup> The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./1st_United_States_Congress" title="1st United States Congress" id="mwKQ">1st Congress</a> met at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Federal_Hall" title="Federal Hall" id="mwKg">Federal Hall</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./New_York_City" title="New York City" id="mwKw">New York</a>.<sup about="#mwt21" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-2"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-2" id="mwLA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwLQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLg">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLw">]</span></span></a></sup> In 1790, it passed the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Residence_Act" title="Residence Act" id="mwMA">Residence Act</a>, which established the national capital at a site along the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Potomac_River" title="Potomac River" id="mwMQ">Potomac River</a> that would become <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Washington,_D.C." title="Washington, D.C." id="mwMg">Washington, D.C.</a><sup about="#mwt24" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-3"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-3" id="mwMw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwNA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNQ">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNg">]</span></span></a></sup> For the next ten years, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Philadelphia" title="Philadelphia" id="mwNw">Philadelphia</a> served as the temporary capital.<sup about="#mwt25" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:02_4-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":02"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-:02-4" id="mwOA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwOQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOg">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOw">]</span></span></a></sup> There, Congress met at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Congress_Hall" title="Congress Hall" id="mwPA">Congress Hall</a>.<sup about="#mwt28" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-5"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-5" id="mwPQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwPg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPw">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQA">]</span></span></a></sup> On November 17, 1800, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./6th_United_States_Congress" title="6th United States Congress" id="mwQQ">6th United States Congress</a> formally convened in Washington, D.C.<sup about="#mwt31" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:02_4-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":02"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:02-4"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-:02-4" id="mwQg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwQw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwRA">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwRQ">]</span></span></a></sup> Congress has met outside of Washington only twice since: on July 16, 1987, at Independence Hall in Philadelphia, to commemorate the 200th anniversary of ratification of the Constitution;<sup about="#mwt34" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-6"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-6" id="mwRg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwRw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSA">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSQ">]</span></span></a></sup> and at Federal Hall National Memorial in New York on September 6, 2002, to mark the first anniversary of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./September_11_attacks" title="September 11 attacks" id="mwSg">September 11 attacks</a>.<sup about="#mwt37" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-7" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-7"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-7" id="mwSw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwTA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAao">Each state has a capital that serves as the seat of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./State_governments_of_the_United_States" title="State governments of the United States" id="mwAas">its government</a>. Ten of the thirteen original states and 15 other states have changed their capital city at least once; the last state to move its capital city was Oklahoma in 1910.</p>

<p id="mwAaw">In the following table, the "Since" column shows the year that the city began serving as the state's capital (or the capital of the entities that preceded it). The MSA/μSA and CSA columns display the population of the metro area the city is a part of, and should not be construed to mean the population of the city's sphere of influence or that the city is an anchor for the metro area. Fields colored <span style="background-color:#FFFFDD;" id="mwAa0">light yellow</span> denote that the population is a micropolitan statistical area.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwBCU"><h2 id="Insular_area_capitals">Insular area capitals</h2>
<p id="mwBCY">An <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Insular_area" title="Insular area" id="mwBCc">insular area</a> is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Territories_of_the_United_States" title="Territories of the United States" id="mwBCg">United States territory</a> that is neither a part of one of the fifty states nor a part of the District of Columbia, the nation's federal district. Those insular areas with territorial capitals are listed below.

Most government functions are based in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Capitol_Hill,_Saipan" title="Capitol Hill, Saipan" id="mwBFE">Capitol Hill</a> village, except for the judicial branch which is located in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Susupe" title="Susupe" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBFI">Susupe</a>.</td></tr>

<td id="mwBGA">1917</td><td id="mwBGE">18,481</td><td id="mwBGI">Like the rest of the U.S. Virgin Islands, Charlotte Amalie (located on the island of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Saint_Thomas,_U.S._Virgin_Islands" title="Saint Thomas, U.S. Virgin Islands" id="mwBGM">Saint Thomas</a>) has no local government and is directly administered by the territorial government. However, it has boundaries defined by the Virgin Islands Code and is recognized as a town by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./U.S._Census_Bureau" title="U.S. Census Bureau" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBGQ">U.S. Census Bureau</a>.</td></tr>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwBGY"><h2 id="Former_national_capitals">Former national capitals</h2>
<p id="mwBGc">Four of the 50 U.S. states — Hawaii, Texas, California, and Vermont — were once <i id="mwBGg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./De_jure" title="De jure" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBGk">de jure</a></i> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Sovereign_state" title="Sovereign state" id="mwBGo">sovereign states</a> with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Diplomatic_recognition" title="Diplomatic recognition" id="mwBGs">diplomatic recognition</a> from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./International_community" title="International community" id="mwBGw">international community</a>. California did not have a capital during its time as a sovereign nation.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwBG0"><h3 id="Hawaii">Hawaii</h3>
<p id="mwBG4">During its history as a sovereign nation (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Hawaiian_Kingdom" title="Hawaiian Kingdom" id="mwBG8">Kingdom of Hawaii</a>, 1795–1893; <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Republic_of_Hawaii" title="Republic of Hawaii" id="mwBHA">Republic of Hawaii</a>, 1894–1898), five sites served as the capital of Hawaii:</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwBHE"><a href="./File:Iolani_Palace_2017.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwBHI"><img resource="./File:Iolani_Palace_2017.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Iolani_Palace_2017.jpg/250px-Iolani_Palace_2017.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="5760" data-file-height="3840" data-file-type="bitmap" height="167" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Iolani_Palace_2017.jpg/500px-Iolani_Palace_2017.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwBHM"/></a><figcaption id="mwBHQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Honolulu" title="Honolulu" id="mwBHU">Honolulu</a> twice served as the national capital of Hawaii and is now the state capital.</figcaption></figure>
<ul id="mwBHY"><li id="mwBHc"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Waikiki" title="Waikiki" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBHg">Waikīkī</a>, 1795–1796</li>
<li id="mwBHk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Hilo,_Hawaii" title="Hilo, Hawaii" id="mwBHo">Hilo</a>, 1796–1803</li>
<li id="mwBHs"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Honolulu" title="Honolulu" id="mwBHw">Honolulu</a>, 1803–1812</li>
<li id="mwBH0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Kailua-Kona,_Hawaii" title="Kailua-Kona, Hawaii" id="mwBH4">Kailua-Kona</a>, 1812–1820</li>
<li id="mwBH8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Lahaina,_Hawaii" title="Lahaina, Hawaii" id="mwBIA">Lahaina</a>, 1820–1845</li>
<li id="mwBIE">Honolulu, 1845–1898</li></ul>
<p id="mwBII"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Newlands_Resolution" title="Newlands Resolution" id="mwBIM">Annexed by the United States</a> in 1898, Honolulu remained the capital, first of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Territory_of_Hawaii" title="Territory of Hawaii" id="mwBIQ">Territory</a> of Hawaii (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Hawaiian_Organic_Act" title="Hawaiian Organic Act" id="mwBIU">1900</a>–1959), and then of the state (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Hawaii_Admission_Act" title="Hawaii Admission Act" id="mwBIY">since 1959</a>).</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwBIc"><h3 id="Texas">Texas</h3>
<p id="mwBIg">During its history as a sovereign nation (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Republic_of_Texas" title="Republic of Texas" id="mwBIk">Republic of Texas</a>, 1836–1845), seven sites served as the capital of Texas:</p>
<ul id="mwBIo"><li id="mwBIs"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Washington-on-the-Brazos,_Texas" title="Washington-on-the-Brazos, Texas" id="mwBIw">Washington</a> (now Washington-on-the-Brazos), 1836</li>
<li id="mwBI0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Harrisburg,_Houston" title="Harrisburg, Houston" id="mwBI4">Harrisburg</a> (now part of Houston), 1836</li>
<li id="mwBI8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Galveston,_Texas" title="Galveston, Texas" id="mwBJA">Galveston</a>, 1836</li>
<li id="mwBJE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Velasco,_Texas" title="Velasco, Texas" id="mwBJI">Velasco</a>, 1836</li>
<li id="mwBJM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./West_Columbia,_Texas" title="West Columbia, Texas" id="mwBJQ">West Columbia</a>, 1836</li>
<li id="mwBJU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Houston" title="Houston" id="mwBJY">Houston</a>, 1837–1839</li>
<li id="mwBJc"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Austin,_Texas" title="Austin, Texas" id="mwBJg">Austin</a>, 1839–1845</li></ul>
<p id="mwBJk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Texas_annexation" title="Texas annexation" id="mwBJo">Annexed by the United States</a> in 1845, Austin remains the capital of the state of Texas.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="7" id="mwBJs"><h3 id="Vermont">Vermont</h3>
<p id="mwBJw">Three sites have served as the capital of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Vermont_Republic" title="Vermont Republic" id="mwBJ0">Vermont Republic</a>:</p>
<ul id="mwBJ4"><li id="mwBJ8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Westminster,_Vermont" title="Westminster, Vermont" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBKA">Westminster</a>, 1777</li>
<li id="mwBKE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Windsor,_Vermont" title="Windsor, Vermont" id="mwBKI">Windsor</a>, 1777–1791</li>
<li id="mwBKM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Castleton,_Vermont" title="Castleton, Vermont" id="mwBKQ">Castleton</a>, 1791</li></ul>
<p id="mwBKU">When the state was admitted to the Union in 1791, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Burlington,_Vermont" title="Burlington, Vermont" id="mwBKY">Burlington</a> was chosen to be the capital and it remained as such until 1805, when it was changed to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Montpelier,_Vermont" title="Montpelier, Vermont" id="mwBKc">Montpelier</a>, where it remains today.</p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="8" id="mwBKg"><h2 id="Native_American_capitals">Native American capitals</h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwBKk"><a href="./File:Navajo_Nation_Council_Chamber,_January_2019.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwBKo"><img resource="./File:Navajo_Nation_Council_Chamber,_January_2019.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Navajo_Nation_Council_Chamber%2C_January_2019.jpg/250px-Navajo_Nation_Council_Chamber%2C_January_2019.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="6000" data-file-height="4000" data-file-type="bitmap" height="167" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Navajo_Nation_Council_Chamber%2C_January_2019.jpg/500px-Navajo_Nation_Council_Chamber%2C_January_2019.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwBKs"/></a><figcaption id="mwBKw">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Navajo_Nation_Council_Chamber" title="Navajo Nation Council Chamber" id="mwBK0">Navajo Nation Council Chamber</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Window_Rock,_Arizona" title="Window Rock, Arizona" id="mwBK4">Window Rock, Arizona</a> is the center of government for the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Navajo_Nation" title="Navajo Nation" id="mwBK8">Navajo Nation</a></figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwBLA">Some Native American tribes, in particular the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Five_Civilized_Tribes" title="Five Civilized Tribes" id="mwBLE">Five Civilized Tribes</a>, organized their states with constitutions and capitals in Western style. Others, like the Iroquois, had long-standing, pre-Columbian traditions of a 'capitol' longhouse where wampum and council fires were maintained with special status. Since they did business with the U.S. Federal Government, these capitals can be seen as officially recognized in some sense.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="9" id="mwBLI"><h3 id="Cherokee_Nation">Cherokee Nation</h3>
<ul id="mwBLM"><li id="mwBLQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./New_Echota" title="New Echota" id="mwBLU">New Echota</a> 1825–1832</li></ul>
<p id="mwBLY">New Echota, now near <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Calhoun,_Georgia" title="Calhoun, Georgia" id="mwBLc">Calhoun</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Georgia_(U.S._state)" title="Georgia (U.S. state)" id="mwBLg">Georgia</a>, was founded in 1825, realizing the dream and plans of Cherokee Chief <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Major_Ridge" title="Major Ridge" id="mwBLk">Major Ridge</a>. Major Ridge chose the site because of its centrality in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Cherokee_Nation_(1794–1907)" title="Cherokee Nation (1794–1907)" id="mwBLo">the historic Cherokee Nation</a> which spanned parts of Georgia, North Carolina, Tennessee and Alabama, and because it was near the confluence of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Conasauga_River" title="Conasauga River" id="mwBLs">Conasauga</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Coosawattee_River" title="Coosawattee River" id="mwBLw">Coosawattee</a> rivers. The town's layout was partly inspired by Ridge's many visits to Washington D.C. and to Baltimore, but also invoked traditional themes of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Southeastern_Ceremonial_Complex" title="Southeastern Ceremonial Complex" id="mwBL0">Southeastern ceremonial complex</a>. Complete with the Council House, Supreme Court, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Cherokee_syllabary" title="Cherokee syllabary" id="mwBL4">Cherokee syllabary</a> printing press, and the houses of several of the Nation's constitutional officers, New Echota served as the capital until 1832 when the state of Georgia outlawed Native American assembly in an attempt to undermine the Nation. Thousands of Cherokee would gather in New Echota for the annual National Councils, camping along the nearby rivers and holding long <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Stomp_dance" title="Stomp dance" id="mwBL8">stomp dances</a> in the park-like woods that were typical of many Southeastern Native American settlements.<sup about="#mwt231" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_29-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-:0-29" id="mwBMA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBME"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBMI">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBMM">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwBMQ"><a href="./File:Council_House,_New_Echota,_GA_July_2017.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwBMU"><img resource="./File:Council_House,_New_Echota,_GA_July_2017.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Council_House%2C_New_Echota%2C_GA_July_2017.jpg/250px-Council_House%2C_New_Echota%2C_GA_July_2017.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="2304" data-file-type="bitmap" height="167" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Council_House%2C_New_Echota%2C_GA_July_2017.jpg/500px-Council_House%2C_New_Echota%2C_GA_July_2017.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwBMY"/></a><figcaption id="mwBMc">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./New_Echota" title="New Echota" id="mwBMg">New Echota</a> Council House (since reconstructed)</figcaption></figure>
<ul id="mwBMk"><li id="mwBMo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Red_Clay_State_Historic_Park" title="Red Clay State Historic Park" id="mwBMs">Red Clay</a> 1832–1838</li></ul>
<p id="mwBMw">The Cherokee National council grounds were moved to Red Clay, Tennessee, on the Georgia state line, in order to evade the Georgia state militia. The log cabins, limestone springs, and park-like woods of Red Clay served as the capital until the Cherokee Nation was removed to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Indian_Territory" title="Indian Territory" id="mwBM0">Indian Territory</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Oklahoma" title="Oklahoma" id="mwBM4">Oklahoma</a>) on the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Trail_of_Tears" title="Trail of Tears" id="mwBM8">Trail of Tears</a>.<sup about="#mwt234" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_29-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:0-29"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-:0-29" id="mwBNA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBNE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBNI">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBNM">]</span></span></a></sup></p> 
<ul id="mwBNQ"><li id="mwBNU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Tahlequah,_Oklahoma" title="Tahlequah, Oklahoma" id="mwBNY">Tahlequah</a> 1839–1907, 1938–present</li></ul>
<p id="mwBNc">Tahlequah, in present-day Oklahoma, served as the capital of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Cherokee_Nation_(1794–1907)" title="Cherokee Nation (1794–1907)" id="mwBNg">the original Cherokee Nation</a> after <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Indian_removal" title="Indian removal" id="mwBNk">Removal</a>. After the Civil War, a turbulent period for the Nation which was involved in its own civil war resulting from pervasive anger and disagreements over removal from Georgia, the Cherokee Nation built a new <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Cherokee_National_Capitol" title="Cherokee National Capitol" id="mwBNo">National Capitol</a> in Tahlequah out of brick. The building served as the capitol until 1907, when the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Dawes_Act" title="Dawes Act" id="mwBNs">Dawes Act</a> finally dissolved the Cherokee Nation and Tahlequah became the county seat of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Cherokee_County,_Oklahoma" title="Cherokee County, Oklahoma" id="mwBNw">Cherokee County, Oklahoma</a>. The Cherokee National government was re-established in 1938 and Tahlequah remains the capital of the modern <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Cherokee_Nation" title="Cherokee Nation" id="mwBN0">Cherokee Nation</a>; it is also the capital of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./United_Keetoowah_Band_of_Cherokee_Indians" title="United Keetoowah Band of Cherokee Indians" id="mwBN4">United Keetoowah Band of Cherokee Indians</a>.</p>
<ul id="mwBN8"><li id="mwBOA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Cherokee,_North_Carolina" title="Cherokee, North Carolina" id="mwBOE">Cherokee</a> 20th century–present (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Eastern_Band_of_Cherokee_Indians" title="Eastern Band of Cherokee Indians" id="mwBOI">Eastern Band of Cherokee</a>)</li></ul>
<p id="mwBOM">Approximately four to eight hundred Cherokees escaped removal because they lived on a separated tract, purchased later with the help of Confederate Colonel <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./William_Holland_Thomas" title="William Holland Thomas" id="mwBOQ">William Holland Thomas</a>, along the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Oconaluftee_River" title="Oconaluftee River" id="mwBOU">Oconaluftee River</a> deep in the Smoky Mountains of North Carolina. Some Cherokees fleeing the Federal Army, sent for the "round up", fled to the remote settlements separated from the rest of the Cherokee Territory in Georgia and North Carolina, in order to remain in their homeland.<sup about="#mwt237" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-30" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-30"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-30" id="mwBOY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBOc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBOg">[</span>24<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBOk">]</span></span></a></sup> In the 20th century, their descendants organized as the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Eastern_Band_of_Cherokee_Indians" title="Eastern Band of Cherokee Indians" id="mwBOo">Eastern Band of Cherokee Indians</a>; its capital is at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Cherokee,_North_Carolina" title="Cherokee, North Carolina" id="mwBOs">Cherokee, North Carolina</a>, in the tribally-controlled <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Qualla_Boundary" title="Qualla Boundary" id="mwBOw">Qualla Boundary</a>.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="10" id="mwBO0"><h3 id="Muscogee_Creek_Nation">Muscogee Creek Nation</h3>
<ul id="mwBO4"><li id="mwBO8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Hot_Springs,_Arkansas" title="Hot Springs, Arkansas" id="mwBPA">Hot Springs, Arkansas</a> <abbr title="circa" about="#mwt238" typeof="mw:Transclusion" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Circa","href":"./Template:Circa"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"1837"}},"i":0}}]}' id="mwBPE">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt238" id="mwBPI"><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>1837</span>–1866</li></ul>
<p id="mwBPM">After Removal from their Alabama-Georgia homeland, the Creek national government met near Hot Springs which was then part of their new territory as prescribed in the Treaty of Cusseta. Because some Creeks fought with the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Confederate_States_of_America" title="Confederate States of America" id="mwBPQ">Confederacy</a> in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./American_Civil_War" title="American Civil War" id="mwBPU">American Civil War</a>, the Union forced the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Muscogee_(Creek)_Nation" title="Muscogee (Creek) Nation" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBPY">Creeks</a> to cede over <span about="#mwt239" typeof="mw:Transclusion" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"3000000"},"2":{"wt":"acre"},"3":{"wt":"ha"},"adj":{"wt":"on"}},"i":0}}]}' id="mwBPc">3,000,000-acre (1,200,000</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt239"> </span><span about="#mwt239">ha)</span> - half of their land in what is now Arkansas.<sup about="#mwt242" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-31" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-31"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-31" id="mwBPg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBPk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBPo">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBPs">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
<ul id="mwBPw"><li id="mwBP0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Okmulgee,_Oklahoma" title="Okmulgee, Oklahoma" id="mwBP4">Okmulgee</a> 1867–1906</li></ul>
<p id="mwBP8">Served as the National capital after the American Civil War. It was probably named after <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Ocmulgee_Mounds_National_Historical_Park" title="Ocmulgee Mounds National Historical Park" id="mwBQA">Ocmulgee</a>, on the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Ocmulgee_River" title="Ocmulgee River" id="mwBQE">Ocmulgee river</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Macon,_Georgia" title="Macon, Georgia" id="mwBQI">Macon</a>, a principle <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Coosa_chiefdom" title="Coosa chiefdom" id="mwBQM">Coosa</a> and later <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Muscogee" title="Muscogee" id="mwBQQ">Creek</a> town built with mounds and functioning as part of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Southeastern_Ceremonial_Complex" title="Southeastern Ceremonial Complex" id="mwBQU">Southeastern ceremonial complex</a>. However, there were other traditional Creek "mother-towns" before removal. The Ocmulgee mounds were ceded illegally in 1821 with the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Treaty_of_Indian_Springs_(1821)" title="Treaty of Indian Springs (1821)" id="mwBQY">Treaty of Indian Springs</a>.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="11" id="mwBQc"><h3 id="Iroquois_Confederacy">Iroquois Confederacy</h3>
<ul id="mwBQg"><li id="mwBQk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Onondaga_Reservation" title="Onondaga Reservation" id="mwBQo">Onondaga</a> (Onondaga privilege <abbr title="circa" about="#mwt243" typeof="mw:Transclusion" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Circa","href":"./Template:Circa"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"1450"}},"i":0}}]}' id="mwBQs">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt243" id="mwBQw"><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>1450</span>–present)</li></ul>
<p id="mwBQ0">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Iroquois" title="Iroquois" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBQ4">Iroquois Confederacy</a> or <b id="mwBQ8">Haudenosaunee</b>, which means "People of the Longhouse", was an alliance between the Five and later Six-Nations of Iroquoian language and culture of upstate <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./New_York_(state)" title="New York (state)" id="mwBRA">New York</a>.<sup about="#mwt247" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-32" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-32"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-32" id="mwBRE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBRI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBRM">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBRQ">]</span></span></a></sup> These include the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Seneca_people" title="Seneca people" id="mwBRU">Seneca</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Cayuga_people" title="Cayuga people" id="mwBRY">Cayuga</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Onondaga_people" title="Onondaga people" id="mwBRc">Onondaga</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Oneida_people" title="Oneida people" id="mwBRg">Oneida</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Mohawk_people" title="Mohawk people" id="mwBRk">Mohawk</a>, and, after 1722, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Tuscarora_people" title="Tuscarora people" id="mwBRo">Tuscarora</a> Nations. Since the Confederacy's formation around 1450, the Onondaga Nation has held privilege of hosting the Iroquois Grand Council and the status of Keepers of the Fire and the Wampum —which they still do at the official Longhouse on the Onondaga Reservation.<sup about="#mwt250" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-33" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-33"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-33" id="mwBRs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBRw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBR0">[</span>27<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBR4">]</span></span></a></sup> Now spread over reservations in New York and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Ontario" title="Ontario" id="mwBR8">Ontario</a>, the Six Nations of the Haudenosaunee preserve this arrangement to this day in what they claim to be the "world's oldest representative democracy."<sup about="#mwt253" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-34" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-34"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-34" id="mwBSA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBSE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBSI">[</span>28<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBSM">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<section data-mw-section-id="12" id="mwBSQ"><h4 id="Seneca_Nation_of_Indians">Seneca Nation of Indians</h4>
<ul id="mwBSU"><li id="mwBSY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Jimerson_Town,_New_York" title="Jimerson Town, New York" id="mwBSc">Jimerson Town</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Allegany_Indian_Reservation" title="Allegany Indian Reservation" id="mwBSg">Allegany Reservation</a>)</li>
<li id="mwBSk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Irving,_New_York" title="Irving, New York" id="mwBSo">Irving</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Cattaraugus_Reservation" title="Cattaraugus Reservation" id="mwBSs">Cattaraugus Reservation</a>)</li></ul>

<p id="mwBSw">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Seneca_Nation_of_New_York" title="Seneca Nation of New York" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBS0">Seneca Nation</a> republic was founded in 1848 and has two capitals that rotate responsibilities every two years. Jimerson Town was founded in the 1960s following the formation of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Allegheny_Reservoir" title="Allegheny Reservoir" id="mwBS4">Allegheny Reservoir</a>. The Senecas also have an administrative longhouse in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Steamburg,_New_York" title="Steamburg, New York" id="mwBS8">Steamburg</a> but do not consider that location to be a capital.</p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="13" id="mwBTA"><h3 id="Navajo_Nation">Navajo Nation</h3>
<ul id="mwBTE"><li id="mwBTI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Window_Rock,_Arizona" title="Window Rock, Arizona" id="mwBTM">Window Rock</a></li></ul>
<p id="mwBTQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Window_Rock,_Arizona" title="Window Rock, Arizona" id="mwBTU">Window Rock</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Navajo_language" title="Navajo language" id="mwBTY">Navajo</a>: <i id="mwBTc">Tségháhoodzání</i>), Arizona, is a small city that serves as the seat of government and capital of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Navajo_Nation" title="Navajo Nation" id="mwBTg">Navajo Nation</a> (1936–present), the largest territory of a sovereign Native American nation in North America. It lies within the boundaries of the St. Michaels Chapter, adjacent to the Arizona and New Mexico state line. Window Rock hosts the Navajo Nation governmental campus which contains the Navajo Nation Council, Navajo Nation Supreme Court, the offices of the Navajo Nation President and Vice President, and many Navajo government buildings.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="14" id="mwBTk"><h3 id="Pueblo_Republic">Pueblo Republic</h3>
<ul id="mwBTo"><li id="mwBTs"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Taos_Pueblo" title="Taos Pueblo" id="mwBTw">Taos Pueblo</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Pueblo_republic" title="Pueblo republic" id="mwBT0">Pueblo republic</a></li></ul>
<p id="mwBT4"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Tiwa_people_(Americas)" title="Tiwa people (Americas)" id="mwBT8">Tiwa people</a> settled in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Taos_Valley" title="Taos Valley" id="mwBUA">Taos Valley</a> around 900 CE. They constructed the oldest building still standing around 1000, with more ones built in the late 1200s and early 1300s. Taos was the most populous <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./City-state" title="City-state" id="mwBUE">city-state</a> of all the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Pueblo_peoples" title="Pueblo peoples" id="mwBUI">Pueblo peoples</a> in the 1540s. It is the origin of the younger Tiwa Pueblos of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Picuris_Pueblo" title="Picuris Pueblo" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBUM">Picuris</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Sandia_Pueblo" title="Sandia Pueblo" id="mwBUQ">Sandia</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Isleta_Pueblo" title="Isleta Pueblo" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBUU">Isleta</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Ysleta_del_Sur_Pueblo" title="Ysleta del Sur Pueblo" id="mwBUY">Ysleta del Sur</a>. Taos was the headquarters of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Pueblo_Revolt" title="Pueblo Revolt" id="mwBUc">Pueblo Revolt</a> in 1680 against the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Spanish_Empire" title="Spanish Empire" id="mwBUg">Spanish Empire</a>. After this first successful <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Decolonization" title="Decolonization" id="mwBUk">anti-colonial</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Revolution" title="Revolution" id="mwBUo">revolution</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Americas" title="Americas" id="mwBUs">the Americas</a>, it became the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Capital_city" title="Capital city" id="mwBUw">capital city</a> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Pueblo_republic" title="Pueblo republic" id="mwBU0">Pueblo republic</a>. Taos Pueblo became the capital of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Santa_Fe_de_Nuevo_México" title="Santa Fe de Nuevo México" id="mwBU4">Santa Fe de Nuevo México</a> during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Río_Arriba_Rebellion" title="Río Arriba Rebellion" id="mwBU8">Río Arriba Rebellion</a> of 1837.<sup about="#mwt254" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-35" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-35"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-35" id="mwBVA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBVE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBVI">[</span>29<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBVM">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="15" id="mwBVQ"><h2 id="Unrecognized_national_capitals">Unrecognized national capitals</h2>
<p id="mwBVU">There have been a handful of self-declared or undeclared nations within the current borders of the United States which were never officially recognized as legally independent sovereign entities; however, these nations did have <i id="mwBVY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./De_facto" title="De facto" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBVc">de facto</a></i> control over their respective regions during their existence.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="16" id="mwBVg"><h3 id="Colonies_of_British_America">Colonies of British America</h3>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwBVk"><a href="./File:Old_Albany_City_Hall.png" class="mw-file-description" id="mwBVo"><img resource="./File:Old_Albany_City_Hall.png" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Old_Albany_City_Hall.png/250px-Old_Albany_City_Hall.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="1125" data-file-height="1272" data-file-type="bitmap" height="220" width="195" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/Old_Albany_City_Hall.png/500px-Old_Albany_City_Hall.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwBVs"/></a><figcaption id="mwBVw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Albany_City_Hall" title="Albany City Hall" id="mwBV0">Stadt Huys</a>, the original <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Town_hall" title="Town hall" id="mwBV4">city hall</a> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Albany,_New_York" title="Albany, New York" id="mwBV8">Albany, New York</a> and meeting place of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Albany_Congress" title="Albany Congress" id="mwBWA">Albany Congress</a> in 1754.</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwBWE">Prior to the independence of the United States from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Kingdom_of_Great_Britain" title="Kingdom of Great Britain" id="mwBWI">Great Britain</a>, declared July 4, 1776, in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./United_States_Declaration_of_Independence" title="United States Declaration of Independence" id="mwBWM">Declaration of Independence</a> and ultimately secured in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./American_Revolutionary_War" title="American Revolutionary War" id="mwBWQ">American Revolutionary War</a>, several congresses were convened on behalf of some of the colonies of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./British_America" title="British America" id="mwBWU">British America</a>. However, these bodies did not address the question of independence from England, and therefore did not designate a national capital. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Second_Continental_Congress" title="Second Continental Congress" id="mwBWY">Second Continental Congress</a> encompassed the period during which the United States declared independence but had not yet established a permanent national capital.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="17" id="mwBbo"><h3 id="State_of_Franklin">State of Franklin</h3>
<p id="mwBbs">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./State_of_Franklin" title="State of Franklin" id="mwBbw">State of Franklin</a> was an autonomous, secessionist United States territory created not long after the end of the American Revolution from territory that later was ceded by North Carolina to the federal government. Franklin's territory later became part of the state of Tennessee. Franklin was never officially admitted into the Union of the United States and existed for only four years.</p>
<ul id="mwBb0"><li id="mwBb4"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Jonesborough,_Tennessee" title="Jonesborough, Tennessee" id="mwBb8">Jonesborough, Tennessee</a>, 1784–1785</li>
<li id="mwBcA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Greeneville,_Tennessee" title="Greeneville, Tennessee" id="mwBcE">Greeneville, Tennessee</a>, 1785–1788</li></ul>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="18" id="mwBcI"><h3 id="State_of_Muskogee">State of Muskogee</h3>
<p id="mwBcM">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./State_of_Muskogee" title="State of Muskogee" id="mwBcQ">State of Muskogee</a> was a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Native_Americans_in_the_United_States" title="Native Americans in the United States" id="mwBcU">Native American</a> state in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Spanish_Florida" title="Spanish Florida" id="mwBcY">Spanish Florida</a> created by the Englishman <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./William_Augustus_Bowles" title="William Augustus Bowles" id="mwBcc">William Augustus Bowles</a>, who was its "Director General", author of its Constitution, and designer of its flag.<sup about="#mwt279" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-39" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-39"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-39" id="mwBcg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBck"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBco">[</span>33<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBcs">]</span></span></a></sup> It consisted of several tribes of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Muscogee" title="Muscogee" id="mwBcw">Creeks</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Seminole" title="Seminole" id="mwBc0">Seminoles</a>. It existed from 1799 to 1803. It had one capital:</p>
<ul id="mwBc4"><li id="mwBc8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Miccosukee,_Florida" title="Miccosukee, Florida" id="mwBdA">Miccosukee</a>,<sup about="#mwt282" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-40" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-40"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-40" id="mwBdE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBdI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBdM">[</span>34<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBdQ">]</span></span></a></sup> 1799–1803</li></ul>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="19" id="mwBdU"><h3 id="Republic_of_West_Florida">Republic of West Florida</h3>
<p id="mwBdY">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Republic_of_West_Florida" title="Republic of West Florida" id="mwBdc">Republic of West Florida</a> was a short-lived nation that broke away from the territory of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Spanish_West_Florida" title="Spanish West Florida" id="mwBdg">Spanish West Florida</a> in 1810.  It comprised the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Florida_Parishes" title="Florida Parishes" id="mwBdk">Florida Parishes</a> of the modern state of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Louisiana" title="Louisiana" id="mwBdo">Louisiana</a> and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Mobile_District" title="Mobile District" id="mwBds">Mobile District</a> of the modern states of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Mississippi" title="Mississippi" id="mwBdw">Mississippi</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Alabama" title="Alabama" id="mwBd0">Alabama</a>. (The Republic of West Florida did not include any part of the modern state of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Florida" title="Florida" id="mwBd4">Florida</a>.) Ownership of the area had been in dispute between Spain and the United States, which claimed that it had been included in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Louisiana_Purchase" title="Louisiana Purchase" id="mwBd8">Louisiana Purchase</a> of 1803. Within two months of the settlers' rebellion and the declaration of an independent nation, President <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./James_Madison" title="James Madison" id="mwBeA">James Madison</a> sent American forces to peaceably occupy the new republic. It was formally annexed by the United States in 1812 over the objections of Spain and the land was divided between the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Territory_of_Orleans" title="Territory of Orleans" id="mwBeE">Territory of Orleans</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Mississippi_Territory" title="Mississippi Territory" id="mwBeI">Territory of Mississippi</a>. During its brief existence, the capital of the Republic of West Florida was:</p>
<ul id="mwBeM"><li id="mwBeQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./St._Francisville,_Louisiana" title="St. Francisville, Louisiana" id="mwBeU">St. Francisville, Louisiana</a>, 1810</li></ul>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="20" id="mwBeY"><h3 id="Republic_of_Indian_Stream">Republic of Indian Stream</h3>
<p id="mwBec">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Republic_of_Indian_Stream" title="Republic of Indian Stream" id="mwBeg">Republic of Indian Stream</a> was an unrecognized independent nation within the present state of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./New_Hampshire" title="New Hampshire" id="mwBek">New Hampshire</a>.</p>
<ul id="mwBeo"><li id="mwBes">The area that would become <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Pittsburg,_New_Hampshire" title="Pittsburg, New Hampshire" id="mwBew">Pittsburg, New Hampshire</a>, 1832–1835</li></ul>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="21" id="mwBe0"><h3 id="California_Republic">California Republic</h3>
<p id="mwBe4">Before being annexed by the United States in 1848 (following the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Mexican–American_War" title="Mexican–American War" id="mwBe8">Mexican–American War</a>), a small portion of north-central California declared itself the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./California_Republic" title="California Republic" id="mwBfA">California Republic</a>, in an act of independence from Mexico, in 1846 (see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./California_Republic" title="California Republic" id="mwBfE">Bear Flag Revolt</a>).  The republic only existed a month before it disbanded itself to join the advancing American army; its claimed territory later became part of the United States as a result of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Mexican_Cession" title="Mexican Cession" id="mwBfI">Mexican Cession</a>.</p>

<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwBfM"><a href="./File:Original_Todd_bear_flag.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwBfQ"><img resource="./File:Original_Todd_bear_flag.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Original_Todd_bear_flag.jpg/250px-Original_Todd_bear_flag.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="510" data-file-height="322" data-file-type="bitmap" height="110" width="175" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Original_Todd_bear_flag.jpg/500px-Original_Todd_bear_flag.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwBfU"/></a><figcaption id="mwBfY">The original of Todd's <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Bear_Flag" title="Bear Flag" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBfc">Bear Flag</a>, photographed in 1890</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwBfg"><a href="./File:Flag_of_California.svg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwBfk"><img resource="./File:Flag_of_California.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Flag_of_California.svg/250px-Flag_of_California.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="600" data-file-type="drawing" height="117" width="175" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Flag_of_California.svg/500px-Flag_of_California.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwBfo"/></a><figcaption id="mwBfs">Modern flag of the State of California</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwBfw">The very short-lived California Republic was never recognized by the United States, Mexico or any other nation.  The flag, featuring a silhouette of a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./California_grizzly_bear" title="California grizzly bear" id="mwBf0">California grizzly bear</a>, a star, and the words "California Republic", became known as the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Bear_Flag" title="Bear Flag" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBf4">Bear Flag</a> and was later the basis for the official state flag of California.</p>

<p id="mwBf8">There was one <i id="mwBgA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./De_facto" title="De facto" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBgE">de facto</a></i> capital of the California Republic:</p>
<ul id="mwBgI"><li id="mwBgM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Sonoma,_California" title="Sonoma, California" id="mwBgQ">Sonoma</a>, 1846</li></ul>
<div style="clear:both;" class="" about="#mwt283" typeof="mw:Transclusion" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"clear","href":"./Template:Clear"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}' id="mwBgU"></div>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="22" id="mwBgY"><h3 id="Confederate_States">Confederate States</h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwBgc"><a href="./File:Virginia_State_Capitol_Building_2.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwBgg"><img resource="./File:Virginia_State_Capitol_Building_2.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Virginia_State_Capitol_Building_2.jpg/250px-Virginia_State_Capitol_Building_2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="6000" data-file-height="4000" data-file-type="bitmap" height="167" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Virginia_State_Capitol_Building_2.jpg/500px-Virginia_State_Capitol_Building_2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwBgk"/></a><figcaption id="mwBgo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Richmond,_Virginia" title="Richmond, Virginia" id="mwBgs">Richmond</a> served as the second capital of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Confederate_States_of_America" title="Confederate States of America" id="mwBgw">Confederate States of America</a>. The city has been Virginia's capital since 1780.</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwBg0">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Confederate_States_of_America" title="Confederate States of America" id="mwBg4">Confederate States of America</a> (C.S.A.) had three capitals during its existence. The first capital was established February 4, 1861, in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./History_of_Montgomery,_Alabama#Montgomery_in_the_Civil_War" title="History of Montgomery, Alabama" id="mwBg8">Montgomery</a>, Alabama, and remained there until it was moved to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Richmond_in_the_American_Civil_War" title="Richmond in the American Civil War" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBhA">Richmond</a>, Virginia, on May 29, 1861, after Virginia seceded on May 23.</p>

<p id="mwBhE">The individual state capitals remained the same in the Confederacy as they had been in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Union_(American_Civil_War)" title="Union (American Civil War)" id="mwBhI">Union</a> (U.S.A.), although as the advancing <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Union_Army" title="Union Army" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBhM">Union Army</a> used those cities for military districts, some of the Confederate governments were relocated or moved out of state, traveling along with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Confederate_States_Army" title="Confederate States Army" id="mwBhQ">secessionist armies</a>.</p>

<ul id="mwBhU"><li id="mwBhY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./History_of_Montgomery,_Alabama#Montgomery_in_the_Civil_War" title="History of Montgomery, Alabama" id="mwBhc">Montgomery, Alabama</a>, February 4, 1861 – May 29, 1861</li>
<li id="mwBhg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Richmond_in_the_American_Civil_War" title="Richmond in the American Civil War" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBhk">Richmond, Virginia</a>, May 29, 1861 – April 3, 1865</li>
<li id="mwBho"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Danville,_Virginia" title="Danville, Virginia" id="mwBhs">Danville, Virginia</a>, April 2, 1865- May 10, 1865<sup about="#mwt286" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-41" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-41"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-41" id="mwBhw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBh0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBh4">[</span>35<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBh8">]</span></span></a></sup></li></ul>
<div style="clear:both;" class="" about="#mwt287" typeof="mw:Transclusion" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"clear","href":"./Template:Clear"},"params":{},"i":0}}]}' id="mwBiA"></div>

<section data-mw-section-id="23" id="mwBiE"><h4 id="Free_State_of_Jones">Free State of Jones</h4>
<p id="mwBiI">In 1863 and 1864, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Jones_County,_Mississippi" title="Jones County, Mississippi" id="mwBiM">Jones County, Mississippi</a> revolted against Confederate rule and became practically independent under the name <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Free_State_of_Jones" title="Free State of Jones" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBiQ">Free State of Jones</a>. The Free State fought a number of skirmishes with Confederate troops. By the spring of 1864 the Jones County rebels had taken effective control of the county from the Confederate government, raised an American flag over the courthouse in Ellisville, and sent a letter to Union General William T. Sherman declaring Jones County's independence from the Confederacy.<sup about="#mwt290" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-mshistorynow.mdah.ms.gov_42-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"mshistorynow.mdah.ms.gov"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-mshistorynow.mdah.ms.gov-42"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-mshistorynow.mdah.ms.gov-42" id="mwBiU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBiY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBic">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBig">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwBik">Scholars have disputed whether the county truly seceded, with some concluding it did not fully secede. Lack of documentation makes the situation difficult to assess. The rebellion in Jones County has been variously characterized as consisting of local skirmishes to being a full-fledged war of independence.<sup about="#mwt291" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-mshistorynow.mdah.ms.gov_42-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"mshistorynow.mdah.ms.gov"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-mshistorynow.mdah.ms.gov-42" id="mwBio"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBis"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBiw">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBi0">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<ul id="mwBi4"><li id="mwBi8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Ellisville,_Mississippi" title="Ellisville, Mississippi" id="mwBjA">Ellisville, Mississippi</a></li></ul>

</section></section></section><section data-mw-section-id="24" id="mwBjE"><h2 id="Historical_state,_colonial,_and_territorial_capitals"><span id="Historical_state.2C_colonial.2C_and_territorial_capitals" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Historical state, colonial, and territorial capitals</h2>
<p id="mwBjI">Most of the original <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Thirteen_Colonies" title="Thirteen Colonies" id="mwBjM">Thirteen Colonies</a> had their capitals occupied or attacked by the British during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./American_Revolutionary_War" title="American Revolutionary War" id="mwBjQ">American Revolutionary War</a>.  State governments operated where and as they could.  The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./New_York_City" title="New York City" id="mwBjU">City of New York</a> was occupied by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./British_Armed_Forces" title="British Armed Forces" id="mwBjY">British troops</a> from 1776 to 1783.  A similar situation occurred during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./War_of_1812" title="War of 1812" id="mwBjc">War of 1812</a>, during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./American_Civil_War" title="American Civil War" id="mwBjg">American Civil War</a> in many <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Confederate_States_of_America" title="Confederate States of America" id="mwBjk">Confederate states</a>, and during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Pueblo_Revolt" title="Pueblo Revolt" id="mwBjo">Pueblo Revolt</a> of 1680<span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwBjs">–</span>1692 in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./New_Mexico" title="New Mexico" id="mwBjw">New Mexico</a>.</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwBj0"><a href="./File:Castillo_de_San_Marco_National_Monument_-_49907540721.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwBj4"><img resource="./File:Castillo_de_San_Marco_National_Monument_-_49907540721.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Castillo_de_San_Marco_National_Monument_-_49907540721.jpg/250px-Castillo_de_San_Marco_National_Monument_-_49907540721.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="2000" data-file-type="bitmap" height="167" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Castillo_de_San_Marco_National_Monument_-_49907540721.jpg/500px-Castillo_de_San_Marco_National_Monument_-_49907540721.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwBj8"/></a><figcaption id="mwBkA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./St._Augustine,_Florida" title="St. Augustine, Florida" id="mwBkE">St. Augustine</a> served as Florida's capital from 1565 until the 1820s.<sup about="#mwt294" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-43" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-43"}}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-43" id="mwBkI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBkM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBkQ">[</span>37<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBkU">]</span></span></a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwBkY">Twenty-two state capitals have been a capital longer than their state has been a state, since they served as the capital of a predecessor territory, colony, or republic. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Boston" title="Boston" id="mwBkc">Boston</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Massachusetts" title="Massachusetts" id="mwBkg">Massachusetts</a>, has been a capital city since 1630; it is the oldest continuously running capital in the United States. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Santa_Fe,_New_Mexico" title="Santa Fe, New Mexico" id="mwBkk">Santa Fe, New Mexico</a>, is the oldest capital city, having become capital in 1610 and though interrupted by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Pueblo_Revolt" title="Pueblo Revolt" id="mwBko">Pueblo Revolt</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Pueblo_republic" title="Pueblo republic" id="mwBks">Pueblo republic</a> from 1680-1692 and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Rio_Arriba_Rebellion" title="Rio Arriba Rebellion" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBkw">Rio Arriba Rebellion</a> of 1837, when the capital was moved to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Taos_Pueblo" title="Taos Pueblo" id="mwBk0">Taos Pueblo</a>, which was established around 1000 is the oldest city in the United States. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./New_Spain" title="New Spain" id="mwBk4">Spanish</a> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./St._Augustine,_Florida" title="St. Augustine, Florida" id="mwBk8">St. Augustine, Florida</a> was the first European settlement in the United States and served as a colonial capital from 1565 until about 1820, more than 250 years.</p>

<p id="mwBlA">The table below includes the following information:</p>
<ol id="mwBlE"><li id="mwBlI">The state, the year in which statehood was granted, and the state's capital are shown in <b id="mwBlM">bold</b> type.  NOTE: For the first thirteen states, formerly the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Thirteen_Colonies" title="Thirteen Colonies" id="mwBlQ">Thirteen Colonies</a> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Kingdom_of_Great_Britain" title="Kingdom of Great Britain" id="mwBlU">Great Britain</a> on the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./East_Coast_of_the_United_States" title="East Coast of the United States" id="mwBlY">Atlantic seaboard</a>, the year of statehood is shown as 1776 (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./United_States_Declaration_of_Independence" title="United States Declaration of Independence" id="mwBlc">United States Declaration of Independence</a>) rather than the subsequent year each state ratified the 1787 <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Constitution_of_the_United_States" title="Constitution of the United States" id="mwBlg">United States Constitution</a>. (See <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./List_of_U.S._states_by_date_of_admission_to_the_Union" title="List of U.S. states by date of admission to the Union" id="mwBlk">List of U.S. states by date of admission to the Union</a>.)</li>
<li id="mwBlo">The year listed for each capital is the starting date; the ending date is the starting date for the successor unless otherwise indicated.</li>
<li id="mwBls">In many cases, capital cities of historical jurisdictions were outside of a state's present borders. (Those cities are generally indicated with the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./List_of_U.S._state_and_territory_abbreviations" title="List of U.S. state and territory abbreviations" id="mwBlw">two-letter abbreviation</a> for the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./U.S._state" title="U.S. state" id="mwBl0">U.S. state</a> in which the former administrative capital is now located.)</li></ol>

<td id="mwB20"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Arkansas_Post" title="Arkansas Post" id="mwB24">Arkansas Post</a></td><td id="mwB28">1819</td><td rowspan="2" id="mwB3A">Capitals of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="./Arkansas_Territory" title="Arkansas Territory" id="mwB3E">Arkansaw Territory</a>.<sup about="#mwt309" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Arkansas_49-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Arkansas","group":"lower-alpha"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Arkansas-49"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"efn","href":"./Template:Efn"},"params":{"name":{"wt":"Arkansas"},"1":{"wt":"The name [[Arkansas]] has been pronounced and spelled in a variety of fashions.  The region was organized as the [[Territory of Arkansaw]] on July 4, 1819, but the territory was admitted to the [[United States|Union]] as the [[Arkansas|State of Arkansas]] on June 15, 1836.  The name was historically pronounced {{IPAc-en|ˈ|ɑr|k|ə|n|s|ɔː}}, {{IPAc-en|ɑr|ˈ|k|æ|n|z|ə|s}}, and several other variants.  In 1881, the [[Arkansas General Assembly]] passed the following concurrent resolution (Arkansas Statutes, Title 1, Chapter 4, Section 105):\n&lt;blockquote>Whereas, confusion of practice has arisen in the pronunciation of the name of our state and it is deemed important that the true pronunciation should be determined for use in oral official proceedings.&lt;/blockquote>\n&lt;blockquote>And, whereas, the matter has been thoroughly investigated by the State Historical Society and the Eclectic Society of Little Rock, which have agreed upon the correct pronunciation as derived from history, and the early usage of the American immigrants.&lt;/blockquote>\n&lt;blockquote>Be it therefore resolved by both houses of the General Assembly, that the only true pronunciation of the name of the state, in the opinion of this body, is that received by the French from the Native Americans and committed to writing in the French word representing the sound. It should be pronounced in three (3) syllables, with the final \"s\" silent, the \"a\" in each syllable with the Italian sound, and the accent on the first and last syllables. The pronunciation with the accent on the second syllable with the sound of \"a\" in \"man\" and the sounding of the terminal \"s\" is an innovation to be discouraged.&lt;/blockquote>\nCitizens of the [[Kansas|State of Kansas]] often pronounce the [[Arkansas River]] {{IPAc-en|ɑr|ˈ|k|æ|n|z|ə|s}} in a manner similar to the common pronunciation of the name of their state."}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="./List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States#cite_note-Arkansas-49" data-mw-group="lower-alpha" id="mwB3I"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwB3M"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB3Q">[</span>h<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwB3U">]</span></span></a></sup></td></tr>
